I. Answer, in brief, any SIX questions

a. What is the difference between accurate and precise measurement?
Accurate measurement means capable of providing a correct reading or measurement. In physical
science, it means correct. A measurement is accurate if it correctly reflects the size of the thing being
measured.
Precise measurement means exact, as in performance, execution, or amount. In physical science it
means repeatable, reliable, getting the same measurement each time.
b. A ball having a momentum p hits a bat and its momentum becomes — p. What is the change in momentum of
the ball?
From the question, we have
Initial momentum of the ball before hitting = P
Final momentum of the ball after hitting = - P
Thus, the change in momentum = Final momentum - initial momentum
-21)
Thus, the change in momentum after hitting by bat will be 2? (in magnitude).
c. State the conditions of equilibrium of a system of coplanar force.
The coplanar forces remain in equilibrium if the vector sum of all the forces is zero. I.e.,
EF = 0 where F s are coplanar forces. At this condition the total torque is also zero.
d. Does the acceleration ofa body moving in a circular path remain constant?
No, the acceleration of a body moving in a circular path does not remain constant. Although, the
magnitude of acceleration of the body moving in a circular path remains constant, its direction
changes with time. Being acceleration a vector quantity, it has both magnitude and direction. Due to
change in direction, the acceleration changes with time in a circular path but not remain constant.
e. Sand is thrown on the roads covered with snow. Why?
Sand is thrown on the roads covered with snow because it increases friction. When the sand is
thrown in• the icy road, it increases the coefficient of friction between the tyres of the vehicles and the
road which prevents the sliding of vehicles.
f. Since the moon is constantly attracted towards the earth by the gravitational interaction, why doesn't it
crash into the earth?
The moon is continuously and constantly attracted towards the earth by the gravitational interaction
between the earth and moon. AAt the same time, the moon is also trying to move straight ahead
because of its speed and the inertia of direction. So, its motion is tangential to the circular path. Due
to this, the gravitational force and the motion are perpendicular to each other and the work done by
this force is thus, W = F g = FScos 0=0 being 0 = 900, cos90 = 0. Therefore, the moon does not crash
onto the earth.20 A COMPLETE NEB SOLUTION TO PHYSICS •Xl,

Answer, in brief, any ONE question:
4.
A man inside an insulated metallic cage does not receive shock when the cage is highly charged. Explain.
a.
The potential at each point inside cage is same as that of the cage itself so that there is no potential
difference between the man and the highly charged cage. Thus, the man does not receive any electric
shock.
b. Is it possible to charge a capacitor to any potential?
No, we cannot charge a capacitor to -any potential. It is limited by the dielectric strength of the
medium between the 'two plates of the capacitor. If we apply the electric potential greater than its
dielectric strength, its insulation will break and charges cannot be stored. So, we can charge a
capacitor to a potential less than the dielectric strength of the insulator kept between the plates of
capacitor.

a. State the principle of conservation of linear momentum. How does NevRon's third law of motion lead to the
principle of conservation of linear momentum?
Statement: The principle of the conservation of linear momentum is stated as; "the total linear
momentum of a system remains constant if no external forces act on the system." Or it states that "the
total linear momentum of the system remains same before and after collision if there is no external force
applied." For example, when a bullet is fired in the forward direction, there is recoil of the rifle in the
backward direction so that the total linear momentum before and after firing is zero and hence, conserve
the momentum.
Proof:

EA = EB Ec = mgh. Thus, when a body falls freely under the action of gravity, the kinetic energy and
potential energy Vary from point to point during motion but the total mechanical energy (K.E + P.E.)
remains constant at each point. So, the mechanical energy is conserved when the. body falls under the
action of gravity.
Conservative and Non-Conservative Forces:
A force is said to be conservative if the total work done by it on moving a particle roun a closed path
is zero. The work done by it in moving a particle from one point to another point depends only on the
distance between these points but not the actual path followed. For example, gravitational forcev
A force is said to be non-conservative if the work done by it on moving a particle round a closed path
is not zero. The work done by non-conservative force depends upon the path followed and initial and
final position also. For example, frictional force.

Define specific latent heat of fusion of a solid. Develop an expression for determination of the latent heat of
fusion of ice.
Specific Latent heat of fusion of a solid: The amount of heat required to change unit mass of solid to
liquid at its melting point is called Specific latent heat of fusion of solid.
Latent heat of fusion of ice by the method of mixture:
Let us take a clean and dry calorimeter with stirrer and weighted.
The calorimeter is filled with water about one third and weighted again. The calorimeter is now
placed into the wooden box and initial temperature is noted.
Small pieces of ice are taken and water in them is soaked with blotting paper and then dropped
gently into the calorimeter containing water. The mixture is stirred gently until the temperature of
mixture falls down and becomes constant. The final temperature of mixture is noted and weighted
lhe mixture again.
Suppose,
Mass of calorimeter and stirrer = mc
Mass of calorimeter stirrer and water = mi
Mass of water taken = mi — mc = mw
Initial temperature of calorimeter and
Mass of calorimeter, stirrer, water and ice = nu
Mass of ice taken = nu — nil = ml
Final temperature of mixture = 0
Specific heat capacity of water= sw
Specific heat capacity of calorimeter system = Sc
Latent heat of fusion of ice = L
Heat gained by the melted ice being heated from OOC to the constant final temperature OOC = mt sw
(0-0) = SWO
Thus, the total heat gained = m,Ll + SWO
The heat lost by the calorimeter system and water

From the principle of calorimetry,
Heat gained by ice = heat lost by calorimeter system and water

A Carnot engine is a theoretical engine, which is used to. convert heat into mechanical work' It is
free from all the defeéG of practicalængines. Its efficiency is maximum and it is an ideal heat
engine. A Carnot heat engine consists of,
Source: It has fixed high temperature (Tl) from which the heatengine can draw heat and has infinite
thermal capacity,
Sink: It has fixed low 'temperature (T2) to which any amount of heat can be rejected and has infinite

6.
Describe the construction and working of a compound microscope and hence, derive an expression for its
magnifying power.
Compound microscope: A microscope which is a combination of two convex lenses of short focal
length and has a larger magnification is called the compound microscope. A lens of short aperture
and short focal length facing the object is called objective lens. And, another lens of short focal length
and large aperture lying towards eye is called eye lens.
h
Eye

Principle: When a smpll object is placed just outside the focus of the objective lens, its real, invertec
and magnified image is produced on the other side of the lens. The image produced by the
lens acts as object for eye lens. The position of eye lens is so adjusted that the finaFimage is formed a

Distinguish between precise and accurate measurements.
Accurate measurement means capable of providing a correct reading or measurement. In physical
science, it means correct. A measurement is accurate if it correctly reflects the size of the thing being
measured.
Precise measurement means exact, as in performance, execution, or amount. In physical science it
means repeatable, reliable, getting the same measurement each time.
3• between accuracy and precession of measurement.

18• What are the limitations of dimensional analysis?
The limitations of dimensional analysis are given below.
It does not give any _information about the dimensionless constant.
a.
b. Ifa physiéal quantity depends on more than three factors having dimension, the formula canno
be derived.
c. We cannot derive the formulae containing trigonometric function, exponential
logarithmic function etc.
d. It does not tell whether a physical quantity is a scalar or vector.
e. The dimensionally correct relation may not be physically c0rrect.

11. Is a physical quanüty having magnitude and direction necessarily a vector quantity?
Explain.
& No, a physical quantity having magnitude & direction is not necessarily a vector quantity because for
a physical quantity to be a vector quantity, it should have magnitude and direction and it must follow •
vector algebra like vector addition, subtraction and multiplication. For example, Electric current
which has both magnitude and direction but it cannot be added vectorically so it is not a vector
quanåty.

Is it possible to get a body accelerated even if it is moving with a uriiform speed? Justify. [21
Yes, it is possible to get a body accelerated even it is moving with uniform speed. In case of body
revolving in a circular path with uniform speed, the magnitude of velocity (i.e. speed) in each and.
every point of its path is constant but the direction changes and directed towards centreof circle i.e.
centripetal acceleration is acting on a body moving in• a circular -path åt uniform speed.

Give with an example a case where the velocity of an object is zero but its acceleration is not
zero.
When, an object is thrown up, then at the highest point, velocity is zero but acceleration due to
gravity is g. Also at the extreme point of the simple pendulum or simple harmonic motion, the
velocity is zero but not acceleration.
Because of air resistance, two objects of unequal mass do not fall at precisely the same rate. If
30.

A body is moving with constant velocity v along a circular path of radius r. How much
work is done by the centripetal force in revolving the body?
A body is moving with constant velocity v along a circular path df radius r. work done by the
centripetal force is zero as the force and displacement of body at each point are perpendicular to each
other. The work done is given as, W = F . s = Fs cos 0. Since 0 = 900, W = 0. So, no work is done by
centripetal force.
3. Distinguish
conservative and non-conservative forces.
A force. is said to be conservative if the total work done by it on moving a particle round a closed path
is zero. The work done by it in moving a particle from one point to•another point depends only on the
distance between these points but not the actual path followed. For example; gravitational force.
A force is said to be non-coriservative if the work done by it on moving a particle round a closed path
is not zero. The work done by non-conservative force depends upon the path followed and initial and
final position •also. For example, frictional force.

12. "The earth moving round the sun in an orbit is acted upon by a force, hence, the work must

If a moving bullet striking a block of wood on a frictionless table embeds inside it, what
14. 2058 Q.No. 1
happens to the KE of the bullet?
If a moving bullet striking a block of wood on a frictionless table embeds inside it, the velocity of
bullet decreases and hence, the kinetic energy. of the bullet also decreases. This is because some of the
kinetic energy is utilized.in creating the hole where the bullet gets embedded as given by the relation
KE = 1/2mv2 = F •x S, where S is the distance travelled. In addition, some energy is spent in creating
sound duringsollision and associtated heat.

Centriptal Force
Ifa body moves in a circular path with å constant velocity, has a constant linear speed aid
the circumference of circular path but the direction of velocity continuously change at every point'
the circle and hence, it has acceleration which is directed towards the centre of the circle.
magnitude of the speed remains the same. This implies that if a body is moving with uniform
in a circle, force is acting on the•body at every point on the circles-towards centre of the
force that acts on body moving in circle towards the centre of the circle is called centripetal force
force is required for the body to keep it in a circular path.

4. An astronaut releases a spoon out of a satellite in the space. Will the spoon fall
earth?
No, the spoon will•not fall on the earth when releasing from a satellite in the space. The gravitationd'

5. weight of a on going from earth to moon? mass
change?
The weight of the body (W = mg) depends upon the acceleration due to gravity (g). The acceleration
due to gravity is maximum at earth surface, decreases as going outer space, minimum at null point
and less significant at moon surface. The weight of the body is also maximum at earth surface'
mininyum at null point and less significant when reaches to .moon surface. The mass of body does
change wherever it has taken in moon or earth or space.

074 set B Q.No.1 What is a black hole? Explain its characteristic nature.
Black hole is the last stage of death of star. It has following characteristics;
• its radius is less than critical radius.
• Its specific gravity is veiy high.
• •Its escape velocity is greater than velqcity of light.
No light escape from its surface and hence, its surface is always black.

10. An astronaut in a space capsule orbiting the earth experiences weightlessness. Why? [2)
The centripetal force necessary to keep the space-ship in an orbit around the earth is provided by the
gravitational force between the earth and the spaceship. Hence, the only force of gravity that an
astronaut in an orbiting space-ship experiences is that which is due to the gravitational force exerted
by the space ship. But the space-ship is small, this force is negligibly small and the astronaut doesn't
experience any gravity. Hence, the astronaut experiences a feeling of weightlessness.

QOOChapter
076 set B Q.No.1 The passengers in a small boat are not allowed to stand, why?
The centre of gravity (CG) ofa body should be as low as possible to be stable. If one stands
boat, the CG of boat raised up which makes it less stable. Due to this reason, the turning b
more possible and passengers become unsafe. Due to this reason, the passengers in a small b
not allowed to stand.
table lamp has a heavy and large base, why?
A body is in stable equilibrium when it is C.G. is in the lowest position as well as the base of the
should be large. If the table lamp has heavy base, its C.G. lies in lower position. Again if the b
table lamp is large, the vertical line drawn from displaced lamp lies within base and the lamp
attain its initial position without failing on thé table. So, the table lamp has a heavy and large base
Why is a cow more stable than a man?
Any body is in stable equilibrium when its C.G. is in the lowest position as well as the base of
body should be large. The base of the cow is larger than the base of the man being four footed
The vertical line drawn from the C.G. passes through the base if the cow IS slightly displaced c
position but not in man. Hence, the cow is more stable than a man.
Why a wrench of longer arm is preferred more?
4.
We know, -torque = force x perpendicular distance from axis of rotation. A smaller force can
large torque (i.e. turning effect) if the wrench has longer arm. If the wrench has shorter arm, we Ip
to apply large for to produce small torque. Therefore, we prefer a wrench of longer arm as
to that of shorter one.

Does the centre of gravity of a solid body always lie within the material of the body? Explain.
No, the centre of gravity (C.G.) may or may not be within the body. In other words, it is possible0J
there may be no matter at the C.G. For example, C.G. of a circular ring, sphere, football, etc., lied
their geometrical centre at which there is-no matter.

'10. ggz.1-E!.E..g.Æ2-.1— Mention the conditions of stable equilibrium.
- The conditions for a body in stable equilibrium are:
fhe C.G. of the body should lie as low as possible.

When four-legged animals walk, they always have three of their legs on the ground at
[21
any instant. Explain why?
A body, to be stable should have large base area and vertical line drawn from centre of gravity
should lie within base. When the three legs of an animal remains on ground while, walking, its base
area increases and becomes stabe. Due •to stability, it does not fall down on the ground easily. So, four
legged animal always have three of their legs on the ground at any instant.while walking.
/12• man carrying a load on his back leans forward. Why should he do so?
When a man carrying a load on his back leans forward, the vertical line through the centie of grqvity;
passes through base. If. he •carries the load straight upon his back, the centre of gravity shifted-
backward and the vertical line through the center of gravity falls outside the base (backward) and the
man will feel difficulty in carrying the •load due to the moment acting on theman by the load which
.12
makes his/her unstable. Hence, a-man carrying a loäd on his back leans forward to be more stable.

16. A man carrying a bucket full of water on his right hand always leans towards his left
hand. Why?
When a man carrying bucket of waterin one side leans opposite side, the vertical line through the
centre Of gravity passes through base. If he carries the bucket straight upon his hand the vertical line
through the center Of gravity falls outside the base and the man will. feel difficulty in Carrying the
load due tothe moment acting on the man by the load. Hence, man carrying a bucket of water on his
hand always leans to the opposite side.
17. During pregnancy, women often develop back pains from leaning backward *ile walking.
Why do they have to walk this way?
For normal woman, centre of gravity lies at the middle of her body. But the stomach of a pregnant
woman bolges out from herbody and so the centre of gravity is shifted in front than normal, due to
which she will have a tendency to fall in forward direction. But, if she leans back, the centre of gravity
will be shifted backward towards the normal position, the vertical line draw from CG lies within base
and she will become more stable. This is why för more stability, a pregnant woman leans backward
whilé walking.

22• a body be in equilibrium if it is in it motion? Explain.
Yes, the body can be in equilibrium if is in motion also. From Newton's second law of motion,
If, F = 0, so 0 = ma o s v = constant. This meanS the body is either at rest or in a
linear motion. Hence, the body is in equilibrium if the external force on the body is zero.Thus,
body is in translational equilibrium, it is either at rest or it is moving with constant velocity.

24• State principle of moment and give an example,
If a body is in equilibrium under the action of number of forces, at different points of the body,
the algebraic sum of the moment of all the forces about any point is zero i.e. clock wise momentm
be equal to anticlockwise moment, which is called principle of moment.

16• Explain why spokes are fitted in the cycle wheel.
The spokes of cycle wheel increases its mass at rim and hence increases •its moment of
greater the moment of inertia, the more is the oppositidn •to any change in• uniform rotational
As a result, cycle runs smoothly and steadily. If the cycle wheels had no spokes, the cycle
driven jerky and hence unsafe.
17. What is the counterpart of the mass and force in rotitional motion?
In linear motion, we have
force = mass x linear acceleration
In rotational motion, we have
torque = (moment of inertia) x angular acceleration
or, t
Hence, thc counterparts of mass. and force in rotational motion are moment M inertia and
respectively because they play the same role in rotational motion as mass and acceleration
motion.

20. Why is it easier to hold dom a 10 kg body in your hand at your side than to hold iti
am extended horizontally?
When a 10 kg body is held in our hand with our arm extended horizontally, the force (weight
mass) and the distance of the force from the rotation axis are at right angle. This prd
maximum torque. But when the body is held at our side, the force always acts through ther
axis, so there is a vey small turning effect about the shoulder joint. Hence, it is more conv
hold down a 10 kg mass in our hand at our side than to hold with our arm extended horizontal

Young's modulus for a material in the laboratory? Explain.
Elastic limit: The extension produced in an elastic body is directly proportional to the
producing it but this proportionality holds the force within a certain limit of deformation
the law of proportionality does not hold. This limit is known as elastic •limit.
Young's modulus of elasticity (Y): It is defined as the ratio of normal stress to longitudinal
Normal stress
i.e. Y =
Longitudinal strain
The SI unit of Young's modulus of elasticity is Nm-2.
Determination of Young's modulus (Y): The magnitude of Y for a material in the form
be determined using the apparatus as shown in figure. A and B are two long thin wires
material suspended from a rigid support. The wire A is kept taut by weight L called dead
attached to lower end which carries a scale M. •V is vernier caliper carried by wire B.
The initial reading on the scale with no load is noted. Now, the wire B is loaded in equal steps
kg and corresponding readings on the vernier. are noted. The loads, W, are gradually remove
steps of 0.5 kg and the vernier readings are again noted. If these readings are nearly the same
readings when the weights were added, this shows that elastic limit was not exceeded.
The magnitude of Young's modulus can be calculated by plotting a graph of the force F (= mg) in m
against dieaverage extension e in meter. A s&aight line passing through the origin is obtained as
figure:
F = mg

Derive an expression for the
energy stored in a stretched string. Define the term energy density of a body under strain.
17.
Energy stored in a stretched wire: When a deformmg force is applied to a wire, it is extended against
restoring force set up in the wire due to its elasticity. Thus, the applied force works against the
restoring force. This work done by deforming force is stored as potential energy in the wire.
To calculate energy stored in a stretched wire, consider a wire of length, L, suspended from point P.
A be the area of cross section of the wire. A mass m, is attached at its lower end, therefore, mg is the
deforming force, F applied to the wire. Let x be extension produced in the wire as in figure. If Y be the
Young's modulus of elasticity of material of the wire, then
YAX
If dx is the further small extension, then small work done, dW is given by
YAx
dW F.dx
(ii)
The total work done by deforming force F in extending the wire from 0 to I is
A Y 12
m
x dx = ¯ . ¯
F=mg
This is the elastic potential energy Stored in the wire. This may be written as,
= — x maximum stretching force x extension
W average force x extension
Equation (iii) also can be written as,
1 YI 1
- 2 LA XL AL
= —x stress x strain x volume of the wire.
w
Thus, the energy per unit volume of wire is called energy density and is given by
— ¯ x stress x strain.

Hooke's Law: It states that within elastic limit, the stress is proportional to strain. i.e., stress oc strain
stress
= constant (E)
strain
where E is called modulus of elasticity or coefficient of elasticity of the elastic body.
Since, strain has no dimension and then dimension of modulus of elasticity is same as that of stress.

[41
Proportional limit and the elastic limit: In a stress strain graph, proportional limit is a point at which
the stress is directly proportional to the strain and the elastic limit is the point beyond the
proportional limit at which the w.ire regains its original length but Hooke's 'law is not followed is
called elastic limit.

25' What do you mean by elastic limit? Derive an expression for the energy stored in
stetched vdre.
[3+11
Elastic limit: The extension produced in an elastic body is directly proportional to the force
producing it but this proportionality holds the force within a certain limit of deformation after which
the law of proportionality does not hold. This limit is known as elastic limit.

3• What do you understand by a second's pendulum? If it is
moon, will it gain or lose time? Why?
Thesimple pendulum whose time period is 2 seconds. is called second pendulum. When it is
moon, the acceleration due to gravity is less in moon, the time period will be more and hence,
second pendulum will loose time.

9. Explain why soldiers are ordered to break steps vhile crossing a bridge.
How does the frequency of vibration of a simple pendulum related with its Soldiers are ordered to break their steps while crossing the bridge because when they march in steps,
the frequency of marching coincides with the natural frequency of the bridge. In this case, resonance
will occur in the bridge and begins tonscilate with maximum amplitude which may cause the
destruction of the bridge. To avoid such a destruction of the bridge, soldiers are ordered to break
their steps while crossing it.
10. 067 su Q.No.1 Why are bells made of metal and not of wood? Explain.
[21
There is high damping Of sound waves in wood but very low damping in metals. If the damping is
high, sound can travel for short distance but-if the damping is less, sound can travel for long
distances for longer duration and sound can be heard at greater distances. Hence, bells are made of138 1 A COMPLETE NEB SOL

14• What are the drawbacks of simple pendulum?
The drawbacks of simple pendulum are:
a. The string used cannot be weightless, perfectly inextensible and flexible.
b. No heavy point mass can be realized in practice.
c. The point by which the string is supported is not perfectly rigid.
d. If angular velocity is large, the formula of time period is not valid. The motion of the sim
pendulum can also become circular rather than linear.
e. te time period cannot be measured in space, where g = O.

aaaChapter 12: Fluid Mechanics
[Fluid Statics, Surface Tension and Viscosity]
A. Fluid Statics
Short Answer Questions
Why do clouds seem to be floating in the sky?
Clouds are tiny particles of water. The upthrust force acting on thc clouds and viscous force on it
balances its weight. As a result, the cloud. particles gain terminal velocity and move with a constant
speed in the sky. So, they seem to be floating in thé sky.
Which one gives the feeling of heaviness in the case of a kilogram of cotton or a kilogram
2.
of lead? Why?
[21
A kilogram of lead gives the feeling of heaviness than a kilogram of cotton because the density of
lead is greater than density of cotton. The one kilogram of cotton occupies more Space than lead of
same mass i.e. volume of one kilogram of cotton is more than lead.
A body floats in a liquid contained in a beaker. The Whole system falls
3.
under gravity. What is the value of upthrust on the body due to liquid?
A body can float in water if the weight of body is equal to upthrust acting on it. But when the whole
system falls under gravity, a = g and we have
Weight of the bodv — upthrust = ma
or, mg — upthrust = ma
or, upthrust = mg — ma
or, upthrust = mg - mg =0
upthrust = 0, hence, uptrrust on the body falling freely under gravity is zero.
4• is the bottom of a ship made heavy? Explain.
(21
When the bottom of a ship is heavy, the centre of gravity (C.G.) of the ship lies below the centre of
buoyancy (C.B) and hence, meta-centre (C.M.) of the displaced liquid and the floating ship comes in
stable condition even its equilibrium position is disturbed. So, this makes the stability of the ship.
5• purity Of the gold can be tested by weighing it in air and water. How?
The purity of the gold can be tested by determining its specific gravity. Although the mass and
volume of a substance varies, the specific gravity remains constant at certain temperatu.re. It is
calculated as,
Weight of gold in air
x specific gravity of water at toc
For gold, specific gravity =
weight of gold in water
This specific gravity is compared to the standard value of specific gravity of pure gold (19.3), we can
test the purity of gold.
6• In still air' a helium filled balloon rises upto a certain height and then stops rising. Why? [21
The atmospheric pressure is greater near the surface of earth and gradually decreases to the height. Since,
the density of helium gas is lower than that of air near the ground. The weight of the ballon will be smaller
than the upthrust acting on it and the balloon rises to a certain height when upthrust is equal to its weight
where the density.of air and helium.is same. Atfter this height, the ballon stops to rise because weight Of
ballon becomes more than uptrust.
In hot air ballooning, a large balloon is filled with air heated by a gas burnerat the
bottom. Why must the air be heated?
The density of hot air is less than the cold air and hence, the ballon becomes light which helps to
move upward. Therefore, the air is heated to let the balloon up in the "y.

57 Q.NO',I What are centre of buoyancy and metacentre?
The position of the centre of gravity of the displaéed fluid is called centre of
Thé buoyani forceor upthrust acts vertically upward from the centre of buoyancy.
Metacénter: When a body floating in a liquid is in equilibrium, the centre of gravity of the body and
both lie in vertical line and form a couple. When a body get slightly tilt, both CG

19• State the laws of floatation.
For a body floating freely in a liquid, the following laws, called laws of flotation, must
i. The weight of the body is equal to the weight of the displaced liquid.
ii. The centre of gravity ofrthe body and the centre of gravity of the displaced liquid are in
vertical line.
20. Lead has a greater density than iron,and both are denser than water. Is the buoyant
lead object greaterthan,ölessthan/orequalto the buoyqntrforce on,an iromobject of the same volume?
We know that,
Upthfust = Weight of water (fluid) displaced
or, U = mass of water displaced (m) x g
or, U = volume of water displaced (V) x- density of water g
or, U = Vpg
If lead and iron objects have equal volume, they displace equal volume of water. Hence, the
(i) indicates that buoyant force on the lead object is same as that on the iron object of same volume
whatever be their densities.
21. Does a ship sink more in river. waterorin sea water? Explain.
Since density of sea water is greater than that of fresh water due "to presence of impurity like
weight of displaced water for same volume is greater for sea water than river water. Therefore, lesq
volume of water is needed to float the same. boatin sea than in river.
22. Steel balls sink in waterbut they don't sinkin mercury. Why?
The density of steel (7800 kgm-3) is greater than density of water (1000 kgm-3) but smaller than densib
of mercury (13600 kgm-3). When steel balls are dipped in water, they sink because upthrust duet
water is less than the weight of the steel balls. But steel balls don't sink in mercury because upthrus
due to mercury is higher than the weight of the steel balls
water?
For a body to float, the density of body should less-than the,density. of liquid: Here,' the-density ofü
is less than the density of water. Due to this reason,. the weight of water displaced by ice is.greau
than weight. So, ice floats in water.

The whole taste pf soup is based on how well soup (hot or Cold) •spreads over th&taste buds of, the
tongue. When temperatureis Iow, the surface tension: of wateris high, so. water molecules
back, which prevents them to reach to the tasté>buds. Thismakes .the taste, incomplete, However

A tiny liquid drop is spherical but a larger drop has oval' shapewWhy?
2.
[21
The shape of a liquid drop depends upon the surface tension and the force of gravity acting on it. The
surface tension depends upon the surface area of the drop whereas the force of gravity depends upon
the volume of the drop. Therefore, the ratio of surface tension to the force of gravity is inversely
proportional to the radius of drop (r). When radius of drop is small, the surface tension dominates the
force of gravity and the drop is nearly spherical. But, when radius of drop is large, force of gravity
dominates thesurface tension and hence; the bigger drops of mercury become oval in shape.
3. is solution a better, cleaning agent than
ordinary water?
[21
The surface tension of soap solutionis less than the ordinary water due to which the water.reaches to
every region of clothes and cleans them. If the surface tension. of water is high; the,w.ateri does not
enter deep inside every part of clothes and cannot remove the dust particle. Due to •less surface
tension soup solution is a better cleansing agent than ordinary water.

is surface of a liquid in a
7.
it is either convex or concave. What may be the reason? Explain.
Itis observed that the surface of a liquid in a capillary tube dipped in it is either convex or concave
The reason behind this is cohesive force of• liquid molecules and adhesive force between liquid
molecule and molecules of tube. If cohesive force between liquid molecules is more, the surface
convex and if the adhesive force between liquid and solid molecules is more, then the surface of
liquid is concave.

What are cohesive and adhessive forces?
[2]
CohesiVe force: The force of attraction between the molecules of the same substance is called force of
Cohesionor, cohesive force. It is maximum in solids, lesser in liquids and the least in gases.
Forceof adhesion: The force of attraction between the molecules of the different substances is called
the forceof adhesion or adhesive force. Force of adhesion is different for different substances. For
example, gum has greater adhesive force for a solid surface than water.

32. causes liquid fall or rise in a capillary tube? Derive an expression for the rise or fallofl
liquid in a capillary tube.
The force of cohesion and adhesion between the molecules determine the rise or fall of liquid
the capillary' tube. If the force of adhesion is dominant, the liquid rises inside the tube while
dominant cohesive force cause the fall of liquid inside the capillary tube.

The main cause of the surface of a liquid as it is under tension is its intermolecular force Of attrao
or the forte of cohesion. The molecules inside the surface of liquid are attracted by all
neighbouring molecules and the resultant force is zero. But the molecule lying on the surface of the
Jiquid is only attracted inward and hence, the surface of liquid acts as under tension.

C. Viscosity
Short Answer Questions
Small air bubbles rise slowly while big bubbles rise rapidly through the liquid. Why? [21
2r2(p -
The terminal velocity of a body through a fluid is given by, v=
This shows that if the
radius increases, the terminal velocity also increases. If the air bubbles are large, they have larger
radius, so the terminal velocity will be more. However, if the radius •is small, as in the case of a small
air bubble, the terminal velocity will be less. Hence, small air bubbles rise slowly while big bubbles
rise rapidly through the liquid.
Why is a suction effect experienced by a person standing close to the platform at a
2.
station when a fast train passes?
When a fast moving train passes to a person standing nearby its track, the air between the person and
the train moves with a high speed. According to the Bernoulli formula, P + äPV2 —
— constant, the air
pressure becomes low •near him/.her compared to the pressure at the other regions including behind
the person. So the air moves from back side of the person to front side pushing him/her on the way.

When a smooth-flowing stream of water comes out of a faucet, it narrows as it falls.
6.
Explain.
W'hen a smooth-flowing stream of wa!er comes out of a faucet, it narrows as it falls. This happens due
to the law of continuity of liquid flow, which states that for an incompressible fluid, the product of
cross-section and velocity of fluid should be constant i.e. av = constant. When the water falls down
its velocity goes on increasing due to gravity and area decreases hence, becomes narrow.

Q.N0.2 Why are light roofs blown off during cyclones or strong wind storms?
windstorm, the velocity of wind suddenly decreases when enters into the houses, but its
13.
Dåring
velocity remains high above the roof. According to Bernoulli's principle, (P + pv2 F. constant) the
pressure on the top of region is lower than that at bottom region. The inside pressure beconies very
much larger than the outside pressure of the roof because the inside air has lower kinetic energy than
that-of air of the roof. This difference of pressure results in an increment of upthrust. As a
result; the roof is lifted up and blown off by the wind.
[21
Q.No.1 Define viscosity. Does it depend on temperature?
The-property of fluid by virtue of which opposes the relative motion of different layers of fluid is
called viscosity. It is measured in terms of coefficient of viscosity (n) as given by Newton's formula: F
dv
'lb SI unit is Ns m-2 and its dimension is [ML-I T-ll. The viscosity of a liquid varies inversely with its
temperature.
15. During certain light roofs are blom off. Why?
•16• Machine parts are jamed in cold day. Why?
Thé•viScosity of a liquid varies inversely with its temperature. As the temperature decreases in
wmtér; the coefficient of viscosity of the lubricant increases. Due to the increase in viscosity of
lubricant; the machine parts are jammed in winter.

foreon die oyect is zero so it moves with a uniform velocity (i.e. terminal velocity).

24' Fishes stay alive in frozen pond in winter. Explain.
Fishes stay alive in frozen pond in winter because of anomalous expansion of water. At
has lowest volume and highest density which remains inside the pond and the ice which h' Watt!
temperature below lies on the surface of pond because it has lower density. This makes the
alive inside the frozen pond in winter.
25• Why mercury is used in thermometer?
Mercury is commonly used as a thermometric substance due to following reasons:
It's boiling point is 3570 C and freezing point - 390 C. Therefore, it can be used over a
i.
range of temperature.
ii. Its.expansion is nearly uniform over the ordinary rangeof temperatures.
iii. It can be easily seen through the glass because it is opaque and shining in colour.
iv. It does not wet the glass.
v. It has low specific heat, so it doesn't take much heat from the body whose temperature
be measured.

29. level initially falls.in a vessel when it is heated. Why?

32• Define the coefficients of real expansion and apparent expansion and hence derive ft
relation between them.
Real and Apparent Expansions of Liquid
Let us consider a glass flask of long stem with uniform small bore and it is
filled with liquid up to level P. When the vessel is heated gently, at first the
vessel expands so that the liquid level lowers to a point Q and after then, the
liquid expands so that it's level rises to a point R above P as shown in figure.
Here, actually, PQ is the expansion of the vessel and QR is the real expansion
of liquid. The difference in real expansion of the liquid VQR and expansion of
the vessel VPQ is called the apparent expansion of the liquid. In figure,
Real expansion of liquid= apparent expansion of liquid + of the
vessel
Coefficient of Real Expansion
It is defined as the ratio ofthe real increase in volume of the liquid to the original volume per unit rise
in temperature. It is denoted by yr and is given by,
real increase in volume
original volume x rise in temp. ¯ V x AO
Coefficient of Apparent Expansion
It is defined as the ratio of the apparent increase in volume of the 'liquid to the original pe

volume x rise in temp. V x AO

& Determination of Coefficient of Linear Expansion of a Solid by Pullinger's Apparatus
The experimental method to determine the linear expensivity of a solid consist of uniform
brass or steel) rod AB whose coefficient of linear expansion is to be determined, a hollow metal tube
with steam inlet and outlet, a thermometer, a spherometer, a galvanometer and heating system Which
are arranged as shown in figure.
Let, 11 be the initial length of metal rod at room temperature say 01. When it is placed inside the
metallic tube and heated its length increases, When central leg of the spherometer is lowered to r
the spherometer reading is RI. Steam is passed inside the tube continuously until the
records a constant temperature 92. After the steady state is reached, the spherometer screw is rotated
down so that it comes in contact with the end A. Let thiS reading of•the spherometer be R2.
We have,
Initial length of the rod = 11

In this way, the coefficient of linear expansion of the material of the rod can be determined. The
cubical expansivity of the solid can be determined by multiplying the linear expansivity by 3. If y be
the cubical expansivity of that material, theny = 3u.

When a body is converted from liquid to vapour state, much more energy is required than th

Why does steam at 1000C give more severe burn than water at the same temperature ?
The amount of heat contained in steam at is 540 cal/gm (latent heat of vaporization)
that of water at 1000C. So, when we touch the steam, more heat flows to our body than from
both being at 1000C. Hence, steam at 1000C produce more severe burn than at the
temperature.
3• are fried along with sand, why?
The specific heat capacity of sand is low ite. its temperature rapidly increases with small increase.t
heat. When the groundnut fried along with sand, the groundnut gets fried fastly with
supplied due to greater temperature.
Why is the groundnut fried along with sand?

5. Water remains cool in earthen pots than in metal pots in summer. Explain why?
The earthen pot consists of a large number of small pores. Through these pores; water coines,
is evaporated. For the evaporation, heat is absorbed from the water inside the pot. Since, the
inside the pot has lost heat, it remains cold in the earthen pot during the summer.
6. medical officer prescribes to put wet cloths on the forehead of a person sufferingfron
high fever, why?
Water has large specific heat capacity. When a wet cloth is kept on the forehead of a persol)il
absorbs large amount of heat from the person's forehead without rising its temperature and
temperature of the high fever person falls down quickly. Thus, the person feels relief On loosing hed
Therefore, during high fever, a wet cloth is kept on the forehead of a person.
7• does the temperature of ice fall when some salt is added to it?
When salt is-added to the ice, the ice provides latent heat to the salt to melt. Since, the ice loses heatib
temperature falls.
8• If you add heat to an object, do you necessarily increases its temperature? Justiti
your.
No, the temperature of the object is not necessarily increased if the object is heated. As an example'
the ice. gets melted when it is heated at zero degree without gaining its temperature i.e. at latent
of fusion of ice and similarly at the latent heat of vaporization.

10• do you feel cool in the mouth when you eat halls?
This
Halls needs certain heat energy to melt when it is kept into the mouth, it takes some energy •
means our body losses some heat energy to melt the halls. Since mouth losses heat, we feel cold•
ld. a food cook faster in pressure cooker than open vessel
[2]
water?
When the amount of water vapor and hence, water vapor pressure above the surface Of watei
increases, the boiling point of the water also increases. Pressure cooker is a closed vessel. When water
is heated in it, pressure of water vapor above the surface of water increases, so boiling point Of water
Fises( since a liquid boils when its vapour pressure becomes equal to the pressure above its surface)
above {1000C. It- means, the same mass of the water contains more amount of heat. As a result'
food is cooked faster in a pressure cooker.

9• DO you expect the gas in cooking gas cylinder to obey the ideal gas equation?
No, the gas in cooking gas cylinder does not obey the ideal gas equation. To obey the ideal
equation, the temperature of gas*should be high and pressure should be low. But the gas in a cookih
gas cylinder has high pressure and low temperature. So, it does not obey the ideal gas equation
10• ÄWhat are the characteristics of a gas to be an ideal?
According to kinetic theory of gas, a gas should have the characteristics to be an ideal,
a. The size of molecules should be negligibly small in comparison to distance between them
b. The molecules of gas should alike in size and mass but differ from the molecules of
c. The force of at-fraction between molecules should be negligible.
d. The gas molecules should be a perfect elastic sphere.
e. The temperature of gas should be high and pressure should be low.

12. Absolute zero temperature is not zero energy temperature. Explain.
According to kinetic theory Of ga9es, the kinetic energy of the gas molecules is — mü2 = —KT, wherei
is root means square velocity of the molecules of gas and K is Boltzmann constant. At absolute zero
temperature, T = 0K, kinetic energy of gas is zero, However, all the molecules remain at rest and
exist potential energy. The total energy is the sum of kinetic and potentiarenergy. At 0K the
energy is non zero due to its potential energy. So, absolute zero temperature is not zero
temperature.
13• In the kinetic theory Of gases, why do we not take into account the changes in gravitationj
potential energy of the molecule?
[2]
We do not take into account the change in gravitational potential energy in the kinetic theory
because the gas molecules are very small in size and its gravitational potenüal energy( U r
is negligible comparéd to the kinetic energy (Ek = — mü2 ).
14• Write the unit Of the universal gas constant and give its physical meaning.
Please refer to
do the gases Obey more gas equation
The equation of state of ideal gas is given by: PV = RT, where R = 8.314J/mole K, is called universal
gas constant. The real gases obey more strictly the gas equation PV = RT only (a) at high temperature
and (b) at low pressure.

What do you mean by ideal gas? Derive Pv = mrT, where symbols have usual

feel cold. However, when wood is touched, wood does not accept heat from the body, because it is
the food would remain warm for a. long time. The shiny surface reflects heat radiations falling on it'

31. What is radiation and how does this mode of heat transfer differ from conduction and
Convection?
ItiS the.process in which heat is transmitted from one place to another directly without intervening
medium, Heat radiation comes to the earth from the sun through this process. Heat radiations can
pässlthiougli the vacuum also. Their properties are similar to light radiations. Heat radiations also
form apart of the electro magnetic spectrum.
Difference between Conduction, Convection and Radiation are:
There-are.following differences between conduction, convection and radiation.

016 set c Q.No.2 Why does the temperature of a gas drop during an adiabatic expansion?
According to first law of thermodynamics, the amount of heat given to the gas is equal to the sum of
the change in internal energy of gas and work done by the gas i.e. dQ = dU + dW. During adiabatic
expansion, dQ = O, i.e., dU = -dW, i.e., internal energy of the gas is used to do external work. So,
internal energy decreases. The internal energy of the system is function of temperature, the decrease.
in internal ener means decrease in tem erature.

At constant volume, dQ = dU, so entire supplied heat is used up in raising temperature (internal
energy). At constant pressure, heat supplied does an external work in addition to rise in internal
energy. So, in making specific heat at constant pressure is high for same rise in -temperature as
achieved by the process at constant volume. The relation is given by Cp • Cv = R, where Cp, Cv.
Molar heat capacities at constant pressure and volume and R = Universal gas constant. When a gas is

Therefore, specific heat of a gas can be defined either at constant pressure or at constant volume and

The first law of thermodynamics explains the conservation of energy in thermodynamic system. If
some energy dQ is supplied to a system, then some of it is used to increase internal energy dU and
some energy is used to do external work dW, then from first law of thermodynamics, dQ = dU + dW.

072 Set c Q.No. 2 Why specific heat capacity of gas at constant pressure is greater than its specific heat
capacity at constant volume?
Cv are molar heat capacities at constant pressure and at constant volume respectively.
According to first law Of thermodynamics, the amount of heat given to the gas is equal to the sum of
the change in internal energy Of gas and work done by the gas i.e. dQ = dU + dW. If one mole of gas
IS heated at constant volume, work done is zero and hence, supplied heat is utilized in raising the
temperature of gas i.e. internal energy. However, if one mole of gas is heated at constant pressure, the
gas e Xpands•and hence, performs some work. That is, a part of supplied heat is utilized to raise the
and a part to perform work. Thus, greater amount of heat is required to raise the
of one mole Of gas by IOC at constant pressure than at constant volume. Mathematically,
P Cv+ R, Since R>O, Cp> Cv. Therefore, Cp is greater than Cv.FIRST LAW OF THERMYDYNAMICS •m 241

First Law of Thermodynamics
The first law of thermodynamics is stated as, "The amount of heat supplied to a system is equal to the
sum of the increase in internal energy of the system and external work done by the
Mathematically,
dQ =dU+dW
It is based on the principle of conservation of energy and signifies that heat can only be produced at
the expenditure of mechanical energy„

47' is thermodynamic process? Describe different thermodynamic processes.
Thermodynamic Processes:
The thermodynamic process takes place when any change in thermodynamic system occurs. The
change is due to the change in thermodynamic parameters which are pressure, temperature
volume of the system. The important thermodynamic processes are:
i. Isothermal Process: The process in which pressure and volume of a system change without anv
heat between the system and its surroundings. During isothermal process:• the walls of the cylinder
are perfectly conducting so free exchange of heat between the gas and its surroundings makes
temperature constant in the isothermal process and the expansion and compression of the system is
slow. E.g.,the boiling of water and melting of ice. The equation of state of isothermal process is pv-
constant.
ii. Adiabatic Process: A thermodynamic process in which pressure, volume and temperature of the
system change but there is no exchange of heat between the system and its surrounding is called
adiabatic process.
In. this process, the heat flow can be prevented by surrounding the system with thermally insulating
material or by carrying out the process so quickly that there is no time for appreciable heat flow. eg.,
sudden bursting of tube of bicycle tyre. The equation of state of adiabatic process is PV7=constant,
where y is the ratio of two specific heat capacity of gas.
iii. Isochoric Process: The thermodynamic process in which the volume remains constant is called an
isochoric process. In this process, the working substance is kept in a non expanding chamber. Since
there is no change in volume, the work done in sucha process is zero. Therefore, from the first law of
thermodynamics, we have
or, dQ
Thus, whole of the heat supplied is used to increase the internal energy of the system.
iv. Isobaric Process: The thermodynamic process in which the pressure remains constant is called
isobaric process. This system is carried out in an expanding chamber in which the pressure is kept
constant. So, the temperature and the volume of the system change. The amount of heat dQ given to
the working substance is partly used in increasing the temperature Of the working substance by dT
and partly used in doing external work.
v. Reversible Process: The process which can be retraced in the reverse order, so that it passes exactly
through the same state as in the direct process is called reversible process. Example Of reversible
process is an infinitesimally slow expansion and compression of an ideal gas at a constant pressures
vi. Irreversible Process: The processes which cannot be retraced in the opposite order by reversing
controlling factors are known as irreversible processes. Almost all processes in nature are irreversible
process. Examples, rusting of iron, dissolving of soap in water, decay of matter,.etc•

efficiency than that of petrol engine. It does not have spark plug.
The cylinder of diesel engine is provided with three valves namely air inlet valve I, oil supply valve0
and the exhaust valve E. These valves are opened and closed at proper moments by suitable
mechanism. The engine works on the four-stroke principle.

18• Describe the working principle of petrol engine with the help of its PV diagram

A': Suction
Indicator diagram
The P —V diagram of petrol engine is as shown in figure.
It completes in four strokes. They are:
a. First s&oke (suction stroke): It is represented by NA. As the flywheel .r&tes, the piston attached to it movs
outwards and a partial vacuum üBide the cylinder as shown in fig (i). Due to low pressure I
cylinder, the inlet valve opens and air petrol mixture.is sucked into cylinder.
b. Second stroke (compression stroke): In this stroke, both the inlet valve I and the outlet valve Oare
closed. The mixture undergoes adiabatic compression and its volume is reduced to about th of the
original volume. In P —V diagram it is represented by line AB. The mixture undergoes adiabatic
compression from A to B so that temperature of system rises up to 6000C. After compression, spark
plug produces spark and ignition takes place. It is represented by BC on the indicator diagram. Thß
stroke is shown in Fig(ii).
c. Third stroke (working stroke): Due to the ignition of the mixture, the piston is pushed out with great
force i.e. adiabatic expansion tåkes place as •shown in figure (iii). During adiabatic expansion, the
engine performs some external work. It is represented by CD line in indicator diagram.
d. Fourth stroke (Exhaust stroke): The outlet valve opens due to high pressure and allows the gases to
escape out until the pressure falls to the atmospheric pressure as shown in figure (iv). Heat is repcted
to the surrounding practically at a constant volume. It is represented by curve DA in indicator
diagram. Finally, the burnt gases are forced out of the cylinder with the inward movement
ill
piston. The cycle is completed and ready to start another cycle. It is represented by the curve
indicator diagram.

074 set B Q.No. 3a Use graphical method to locate image of an erect object placed before a concave mirror
between its pole and the focus. Hence write down the nature of image so formed.
When an object AB lies between principal focus and pole of the
mirror, it's image A'B' will be formed behind the mirror which is
virtual, erect and magnified.
073 set c Q.No.3 A concave mirror is often used as an aid for applying cosmetics to the face, why? [21
For shaving or make-up purpose, I suggest concave mirror. When
a concave mirror is used for this purpose, our face or object lies
between focus and optical centre, so that highly enlarged, virtual,
erect image is formed behind the mirror show in diagram.
Convex mirrors are used as rear view mirrors in cars, why?
Convex mirrors are used as rear view mirrors m automobiles to see the traffic at the backside. They
provide wider angle of view, although the images are diminished. They make virtual, erect and
diminished image of wide range.
Does focal length change when the curve mirror is dipped in liquid? Why?
No, the focal length of a spherical mirror does not change when it is immersed in water because the
111
it is
focal length of mirror is independent to the medium in which it is kept. As
f = uv
Independent of refractive index of medium. So, the focal length of mirror does not change when it is
kept in water.

070 Su Set B Q.No.3 A spherical mirror is cut in half horizontally. Will an image be formed by the
6.
botom half of the mirror? How?
When we cut a shperical mirror into half horizontally, thc radius of
C Urvature is not changed. The focal length thus remains the same and
the image fonned will be that of the usual case. The only difference
that happens is that the intensity of the images will be reduced as half
Of the part of the mirror is removed.

Can a convex mirror ever form real image? Justify your answer.
Yes, a convex mirror can form a real image if the object is virtual.
(21
That is, if a converging beam is incident on a convex mirror, a real
image is formed in front of it. In such case, the object •distance is
less than the focal length of the mirror.

14• Derive mirror formula for a concave mirror and state the sign convention used.
[4)
Mirror formula: The relationship between focal length of mirror with object distance and image
distance is called mirror formula.
Consider a concave mirror of focal length f which has to relate with object distance u and image
distance v. Let AB be an object placed normally on the principal axis of the mirror. The rays of light
from the object AB, after reflection from concave mirror meet at point B'. So A'B' is the real image of
the object AB. If the aperture of the mirror is small, the point P i.e pole of the mirror coincides with
point C. Since A's ABC and A'BC are similar, so their corresponding sides are proportional.

19. do you mean by principal focus of a convex mirror? Prove mirror formula
mirror and also show that m = - vlu for the same mirror.
Principal Focus: When a narrow beam of light, parallel to the principal axis and close to it is
on curved mirror, after reflection appears to meet at a point, this point is called principal focus.
usually denoted by F.

Short Answer Questions
This is due to refraction (bending) of light while passing from water to air. In such case, object lies in
dépth is always less than real depth and hence, a clear pool of water always aPPears to be tedbY P
than it is actually.
Why does the sun look slightly oval when it is near the horison?
This is due to refraction of light when passes one mediunl to another. The density of the air in th
• atmosphere is greater near the surface of earth than above it. Thus, the magnitude of
increases with decreasing altitude. Due to this, the rays from the lower edge of the sun are bent
than the rays from •the upper edge when situated at honizon. Hence, vertical dicuneter of the sun is
shortened more than the horizontal diameter. That is why, the sun appears flattened at the of
sunset and sunrise because of refraction of light.
3• stick partially dipped in water seems to be bent. Why?
This is due to refraction of light. When a stick is partially dipped in water, due to refraction of light, it
seems to be bent. When light rays pass from denser medium to rarer mediunm, then rays bent away
from the normal. When a stick is placed in water partially, the light rays from different portions of the
dipped part of the stick undergo bending away from the normal to reach the eyes. So, it seems to be
bendmg.
Can total internal reflection be achieved if object originates in rarer medium? Explain with a
4.
diagram to justify your answer.
No, total internal reflection can not be achieved if object originates in rarer
medium. For total internal reflection, the light ray must pass from denser to
rarer
rarer media and angle of incidence in denser medium must be greater than
Q
critical angle so that angle of refraction in rarer medium is 900
denser

A ray of light in air strikes a glass surface. Is there a range of angles for which total internal
5.
reflection takes place?
No, there is not a range of angles for which total internal reflection takes place when a ray of light in
air strikes a glass surface. It is because for total internal reflection, light should travel from denser
medium to rarer medium. But here, air is rarer medium and glass is denser medium. So, this
phenomenon does not happen in this case.
[21
6• does diamond sparkle?
Diamond shines because of total internal reflection of light. The refractive index of diamond is 2.4
and hence, its critical angle is small, 24.60. The surfaces of the diamond are cut such that light in it
undergoes multiple total internal reflections. The light coming out of some faces makes brilliancy Of
the diamond.

016 set c Q.No.3 Under what conditions does a prism produce the angle of minimum deviation? Explain. [2]
The minimum deviation (ömtn) of light occurs when the ray passes symmetrically through the prism
In this case, the angle of enwrgence is equal to the angle of incidence and angle of refracton at two
faces are also equal. The refracted ray through the prism is also parallel to the base of the prism.

What do you understand by minimum deviation in the case of refraction through a
prism? Plot a graph between angles of incidence and the deviation produced.
When the angle of incidence increases, the deviation angle at first
decreases, reaches to a minimum value and again increases. The minimum
value of deviation angle is called minimum deviation angle. It is the
condition at which the deviation angle is minimum and starts to increase
On increasing incident angle beyond this. At minimum deviation condition,
angle of incidence and angle of emergence areequal, the refracting angles
at both faces are. equal and the refracted light ray inside the prism is
i
parallel to base of prism.
066 Old Q. No. 6 State the factors on vhich the deviation produced by a prism depend.
6.
The deviation produced by the prism depends upon the angle of prism, refractive index of a medium,
angle of incidence, color of light ancl the medium around the prism.
060 Q.No.6 What are the advantages of total reflecting prism over plane mirror?
The advantages of total reflecting prism over plane mirror are:
a• When light is reflected from total reflecting prism, whole of the light is totally internally
reflected. However, from plane mirror, only about 90% of 'incident light is reflected.
Therefore, intensity of reflected light is more -in' prism than that of the light reflected from
plane mirror.
b• No multiple reflections take place in total reflecting prism as in plane mirror.
e image is not laterally inverted in case of total reflecting prism as in plane mirror.n t an in usin
d. Use of total reflecting prism rec uc
Ola
It does not require silvering which is one of the advantages.

Which one Of blue and red light will be deviated more by a prism? Give reason for

answer.
When a white light passes through a prism, red light deviates less than
White light
the blue light. This is because the red light has more velocity, longest
wavelength and hence, least refractive index but blue light has less
velocity less wavelength and Inore refractive index. Since, ö = A(kl — 1),

9• Can a concave and a convex lens be combined so that the combination behaves like a
diverging lens? Justify your answer.
Flint glass
Yes, a concave and a convex lens can be combined so that the
rown glass
cornbmation behaves like a diverging lens. When a diverging lens of
flint glass (with more R.I.) and a convex lens of crown glass (with less
R.I.) are combined the combination becomes divergmg lens.
10• What is the cause of dispersion of light?
When white light is passed through a prism, it splits up into its seven-constituent colours because of
their different wavelength in the prism (denser medium). From Cauchy dispersion relation, we have
B
This shows that refractive index of a prism for a particular colour varies inversely with the
wavelength of the colour. The wavelength of red colour is the highest aniong the constituent colours
of white light. So its refractive index is the lowest. Similarly wavelength of violet colour being the
smallest among the constituent colours of white light, its refractive index is the highest. Moreover, the
deviation produced by a small angled prism is given by, = A (p — 1).
As < then (Sr < (Sv. It means red colour is least deviated and the violet colour is most deviated.
Hence, red light appears at the top of the spectrum whereas the violet at the bottom. Hence, the cause
of dispersion of light is the difference in wavelength of light in medium.
11. Draw an outline sketch to show the chromatic aberration in the case of a lens. How can
achromatism be achieved?
[2]
When a white light beam incident on a lens parallel to the
principal axis, it splits into its constituent colour. The focal length
for various colours light is different depending up on the
refractive index of the material. This defect of lens, which is
unable to focus the light of different colours to a single point, is
called chromatic aberration of lens. Achromatisnl is obtained by
combining convex lens of crown glass and concave lens of flint
glass.
12. mirror cannot give rise to chromatic aberration.
Chromatic aberration occurs when light passes through a denser medium like lens, i.e. in refraction
phenomena, because of different focal lengths of lens for different colours (wavelengths). But focal
11
length of mirror
is independent of refractive index of incident light i.e. all the colours are
reflected by same angle. Hence, such defects cannot be seen in a mirror.

What do you mean by accommodation of an eye?
3.
The abilitv of the eve,to bring objects lying at various distances to focus on the retina is called
accommodation of eve. For this, the Image distance is fixed for all objects of different distances. This
is possible if the •focal length of eye changes for different distant objects. The ciliary muscles, by
contracting cause the lens to bulge out and reduces to focal length of eye lens bringing nearby objects
to focus on the retina. IVhen ciliary muscle gets relaxed, the edge.s of the lens are pulled out, the focal
leneth Increases bringing distant objects to focus on the retiana.

12' What are the defects Of vision known to you? How are myopic and hypermetropic defects
removed? Explain with necessary theory.
& When the lens of the eye cannot change its focal length according to the distance of object, then eye
becomes unable to see the object placed at any point with the range of vision. This is called thc defects
of vision and the eye is called defective eye. So the lens of defected eye focuses the ilnage of the object
either in front of the retina or beyond the retina. According to this theory, there are two kinds of
common defects of vision, namely: Shortsightedness (myopia) and Long-sightedness
(hypermetropia).
Short-sightedness (myopia)
It is that defect of vision in which a person can clearly see the objects lying near it but cannot see
distinctly objects lying beyond a certain distance. For this defect, the far point is much near than
infinity
Retina
(R)
Correction for short sight
Short sight
Causes of short-sightedness
This defect arises due to two reasons:
a. Elongation of the eye ball than the normal size and
Shortening of focal length of the lens, when the eye is fully relaxed.
Remedy: Tlüs defect can be removed by placing a concave lens of suitable focal length close to the
eye as shown in figure. The parallel rays of light from infinity after refraction through concave lens
If * is the distance of far point from the eye, then for the concave lens placed before the eye

Long sightnedness (hypermetropia)
The defect of an eve in which it can see the distant objects clearly but not the objects nearer to the eye
is called long sightedness. The near point of such eve lies farther away froin the least distance of
distinct vision. This defect, also called hypermetropia. The rays frotn an object at normal near point
(i.e. 25 cm from the eye) are brought to focus at a point behind the retina as shown in Fig (i)
This defect arises due to two reasons:
a. Shortening of the eve ball than the normal size and
b. Elongation of focal length of the lens.
Remedy: This defect can be remedied by using convex lens of suitable focal length. The light rays
coming from an object lying near to the eye converges by convex lens and further converges by eye lens
and image formed at retina as shown in fig.(ii).
Let x be the distance of near point from the eve. For the convex lens, object distance u = D and image

Chapter 1: Electrostatics
Bits of paper are attracted to an electrified comb, even though they have nd net charge.
076 Set B
How is this possible?
When a comb runs through hair, it gets charged due to friction with "hair. This charged comb when
brought close to paper, it induces opposite charge on paper pieces and attracts to paper even though
they no net charge. If the hair is wet, instead of dry, the fricüon between hair and comb is highli
reduced and hence, comb is less charged. Thus, it cannot attract the paper.
Can two electric lines of force intersect each other? Explain.
We know that, the 'tangent drawn at any point on electric lines of force ves the direction ofelectric
field intensity at that point. If two lines of force intersect at a point, then it demands'two different
directions of electric field intensity at the point of intersection. But; it is impossible: Hence, two lines
of force never intersect to each other.
3. do you mean by quantization of charge? Explain.
The charges on any charged body arg integral multiple of basic unit of charge: niis basic unit of
charge is taken as the charge on an electron or a proton which is equal to +46 x 10=19C Thereforethe
charge on any body is, Q = ± n e where e is the charge on an electron and-n is any integer This
property of charge is called the quantization of electric charge. This means the chargeson thebody
are not in fraction number i.e. they are in whole

In winter season, our body is dry blit not wet, When we remove our clothes from out b(kiy,• the

produced.
metallic chain is used in-the tanker transporting highly inflammable liquid? • [21

60• Are electric potential and electric field vector quantities? Justify with their definitions.
Also obtain an expression for electric potential at a point due to a point charge.
Electric potential: The electric potential at a point inside the electric field is defined as the amount of
work done in moving a unit positive charge (test charge) from infinity to that point.
Electric field: The electric field is defined as the region around the charge where electric force can be
experienced. The electric field intensity E at a point inside the field is defined as the force experienced
by unit positive charge when placed at that point.
9.
They are not vector quantities because they do not obey the laws of vector algébra.

64. is electrostatic induction? Explain with necessary diagrams, a method of charging
a body positively by induction.
Electrostatic Induction
When an uncharged body is taken near to the charged body, the charges are developed on the
uncharged body temporarily. This temporary electrification of a body by bringing a charged body
close to it is called electrostatic induction.
a. Charging a body positively by induction:
Let us consider a negatively charged glass rod taken to near an uncharged body AB which is fitted 011
the insulated stand. This body AB can be charged positively by induction in the following ways:
Step I: Ifa negatively charged glass rod is brought néar a body AB, the end A of the body acquires
bound negative charge and the far end B acquires free negative charges due to induction as
shown in Fig. (i).
ELECTROSTATICS
n.
rod
(i)
(iii)
Figure: Charging a body positively by induction.
Step II: The body, AB is earthed with the help of the metal wire as.shown in Fig.(ii). in this case,
the free negative charge flows to the earth. However the positive charges being the bound
charges do not move in to the earth.
111: The earthing is removed keeping the glass rod still in its positiom The bound pqsifive
Step
charge on end B remains there as shown in Fig.(iii).
Step IV: Finally, the positively charged glass rod is removed away from the body, AB. Thebound-
negative charge spreads over whole surface of the bodyn AB as shown in Fig;
Therefore, the body, AB becomes positively charged by induction.

What is a capacitor? Derive an expression for energy loss, when two charged capacitors
28. like plates together.
are conn
Capacitor: An electric device which is used to store charge is called
capacitor. A capacitor essentially consists of two conducting surfaces
separated by an insulating material called dielectric. The conducting
surfaces are called plates of the capacitor and the dielectrics are mica, air,
paper etc.
A capacitor in an electrical circuit which is denoted as shown in Fig, in which two vertical parallel
lines represent the two conducting plates and a hofizontal line-attached to each plate is called lead
used in connecting to the external circuit.
Expression for energy loss:
When two capacitors charged at different potential differences and are connected by a metallic wire,
there is flow of charge from the capacitor at higher potential to the capacitor at lower potential till the
potential of the two capacitors becomes equal. In this process, the total charge remains constant but
the total energy before joining the capacitors will be greater than that after joining. This means when
two charged capacitors are connected by a wire, there is always loss of energy.
